`docs/vp2_2d.md`:

```md
# VP2 2D layer (HUD, text, menus): what the native renderer draws from

VP2's interface does not use VU1: each 2D element builds its own GIF packets on the EE, in GS context 2, queued on
the DMA list when its layer is drawn. The native port draws the same items from the game's descriptions instead (no
GIF packet read). Addresses are mod001 unless stated. [V] verified in the MIPS or a dump, [I] inferred.

## Framework

- UI manager G2 = *(u32 *)0x35E170 (vtable 0x15A160): four font objects at +0x14/+0x54/+0x94/+0xD4; sheets 'SHI\0' 0x41 /
  0x3D at +0x818 / +0x81C; scale +0x840 / +0x844 (16.0, 14.9333) and offset +0x848 / +0x84C (27648, 29184): GS 12.4
  X = offset + scale * vx on a virtual 640 x 480 screen (GS pixel x = 1728 + vx, y = 1824 + 0.9333 vy); text shadow
  offset +0x850 / +0x854; registered layers +0x85C (head, linked by +0x98 / +0x9C). [V]
- Layers draw in the scene pass after the 3D (pass byte +0x65 = 1), in registration order; every layer's draw goes to
  0x2EC8F0(layer). Layer: +0x08 child list (next = element + 8), +0x40 / +0x44 position, +0xE0 alpha (0..128). [V]
- Element header: +0x18..+0x24 x, y, w, h (layer-relative), +0x28 alpha, +0x2C z, +0x39 visible, +0x3B built (its
  packet cached), +0x3C dirty, +0x3D enabled, +0x6C font, +0x90 colour multiplier (/128); vt +0x20 build. An element is
  drawn when +0x39 && +0x3D && +0x3B (the game's own chain rule). [V]

## Items (the build functions, one call per item)

- Glyph 0x2E7DD0(element, item): item (0x30 bytes) +0x00..0x0C box (x0, y0, x1, y1), +0x10 / +0x14 x / y scale, +0x18 font,
  +0x1C (int) the glyph's width (its UVs: 2 border + that width, 2 border + font +0x14 high; the rest of the cell unused),
  +0x20 colour, +0x24 shadow mode, +0x28 shadow colour, +0x2E glyph index. Pixels: 4 bits a pixel at font+0x10 +
  index * font+0x20, font+0x24 x font+0x28 (32 x 28); palette part (CSA) 1 for the glyph, 1 or 2 for its shadow (the
  'SHI\0' 0x41 sheet's palette). Quad grown by the border font+0x2C * scale; colour x element+0x90 / 128; alpha =
  layer+0xE0 * element+0x28 / 128; shadow offset G2+0x850 / 0x854 (mode 1). [V]
- Fixed-font string 0x2E75E0: 16 x 16 cells of 'SHI\0' 0x41, cell of c = ((c - 1) & 15, (c - 1) >> 4), 12-pixel advance.
- Sprite quad 0x2EB2D0 (f12 layer alpha, a0 sprite = element + 0x50, a1 cursor, a2 position (float x, y), a3 z, t0 colour
  multiplier); quad list 0x2EAC40 (same arguments). Sprite: +0x00 its sheet's 'FIS' file (until uploaded), +0x04 its
  parts' 'DTT' file, +0x08 parts (16 bytes: u0, v0, u1, v1 in 4.12 of the sheet (s = u / 4096), w, h, +0xC CSA),
  +0x0C quads (24 bytes: part, x0, y0, x1, y1, z; x1 / y1 <= 0: the part's size), +0x14 / +0x18 position, +0x1C /
  +0x20 pivot, +0x2C / +0x30 scale, +0x3C rotation (degrees), +0x40..+0x4C colour (+0x4C multiplies the single quad's
  RGB only), +0x50 alpha, +0x54 part (single quad), +0x58 quad count, +0x5A..+0x61 TEX0 (TBP, CBP, TBW, PSM, TW, TH),
  +0x64 CSA (negative: the part's). [V]
- Sheet upload 0x2EBB10(a0 sprite, a1 cursor, a2 TEX0 destination or 0): the texture manager (0x29D8F0) gives the
  sprite's FIS a VRAM block, the TBP goes to the sprite's TEX0 or to a2. [V]
- Texture manager *0x35DE38 (scratchpad): resident list from the byte at +0x2008 (next +0x2008 + e), entry e key
  +0x1008 + 16e tag / +0x8 + 16e index, TEX0 +0x1010 + 16e (TBP low 14 bits). The native port finds a sprite's sheet
  there by its TBP (the save portraits). [V]
- Fixed UI slots outside the manager: TBP 0 'SHI\0' 0x41 (CBP 0x3FFC) and 0x400 'SHI\0' 0x3D (CBP 0x3FF8), set by
  0x2EC190, set up by 0x2F3630(a0 image, a1 its FIS, a2 PSM, a3 DBP of the pixels, t0 of the palette; uploaded by
  0x2F32F0), so the port keys each DBP from the FIS; TBP 0x800 / 0x1000 PSMT4HH 512 x 256 sheets of the menus, TEX0 records built by mod003 (0x4643C0 and
  callers of 0x30C5E0: TBP 0x800, CBP from the menu context +0x98, DTTs from its +0x90): their upload is not found
  yet; compared with the original's load menu, 0x800 holds 'SHI\0' 0x5E (frame ornaments) and 0x1000 'SHI\0' 0x3E
  (stone), loaded with the menu (the field HUD's 0x800 is 'JOHN' 0x276F). 0x2EC130(a0 sprite, a1 DTT, a2 TEX0 record) gives a sprite parts + TEX0. [V]
- Window background 0x2E9DD0(a0 window): rectangle +0x18 (or +0xB8 when +0xE8) plus its layer's (+0x14) position; one
  gouraud strip (0x44211B, 0x3F1B17, 0x513A24; darker set when +0xF5) at 0.95 x alpha, then 8 strips from the table at
  0x357900 ({x, y, colour} x 4 over the corners x0, x0 + m, x1 - m, x1; m = size / 4, at most 24) shading the edges
  (black at half the alpha to nothing). [V]
- Windows 0x2E9580 (gradient 0x2E9DD0, border 0x2E9C20), scissor 0x2EF440, rectangles 0x460EA0 (mod003), 0x30ED70,
  flat rectangles main 0x120A00 / 0x120870, screen fade mod003 0x3D36C0 (type +0xF3, colours +0x98..+0xA0). [V]
- Sheets are FIS files with their own keys (FIS +0x14 tag, +0x18 index): 'SHI\0' (tag 0x00494853) 0x41 and 0x3D
  (icons, fixed font, glyph palettes), the field HUD sheet 'JOHN' (0x4E484F4A) 0x276F. [V]

## Text encoding

Byte = ASCII - 0x1F; b0 & 0x80 starts a two-byte code (b0 & 0x7F) | b1 << 7, >= 0x4000 a control (0x4000 newline,
0x4001 end, 0x4002 wait, 0x4019 button icon). [V]

## Native plan

Capture each item at build (the game caches the packet and frees the items), keyed by its element; draw an element's
items when its layer is drawn (0x2EC8F0) and the element is shown, in the layer's order, after the 3D.

## Field HUD (2026-11-01)

- Button icons in text (control 0x4019): sprites of the text element (+0x100 -> +0x24, 0x70 bytes each, +0x28 of
  them, shown when +0x66), drawn by 0x2EFCD0 from the text build 0x2EFA30 with direct calls; placed from the layer's
  position plus the element's (+0x18 / +0x1C) less its scroll (+0x78 / +0x7C). The port captures them after 0x2EFA30.
- Prompt backing bar: class 0x165BB0 (mod003 build 0x448AF0), embedded at owner + 0x230 (the prompt layer): a cap (part
  22) and a body to the right edge (part 23) of the field HUD sheet (TBP 0x800 PSMT8H, CBP 0x3FF4: 'JOHN' 0x276F),
  parts at *(*(*(0x48DB48) + 0x5C) + 0x224); left at layer x + e+0x58 + 5, top at layer y + e+0x5C + 2, h + 4 high
  (h part 22's), cap h GS pixels wide (x 0.75 when bit 4 of *(*(0x48DB4C) + 3)). Drawn each frame from live data.
- TBP 0x800 is shared: the menus' sheets use CBP 0x3FC8 / 0x3FCC, the field HUD's CBP 0x3FF4.
- The minimap is another element, not found yet.

## Field minimap (2026-11-01)

The area map object M = *(*(*(0x48DB48) + 8) + 0xC8) (mod003 class 0x165470; per-frame update 0x440FA0, vtable +0x14)
builds the minimap each frame (0x457BE0) as a small 3D scene through the generic polygon batcher (flush 0x200DE0),
plus an EE-built background (X = *(M+0x1640), class 0x167180). Its own 2D frame: origin X+0x94 / +0x98 (1792, 1808),
scissor 24..179 x 24..139 (*(M+0x1650) +0xA4..). The native port draws it from M's data (vp2s_minimap.cpp): the
background and border, the player's layer of rooms (records *(M+0x1644), 0xF0 bytes, textured with the floor image
*(M+0x29C)) through the scene object (*(M+0x1630) +0x90) and the map camera (*(M+0x1638)), after the 3D and before
the effect passes (it is part of the scene: doubled and scattered like it). Not yet: visited rooms only, doors,
icons, the player's marker (a green spinning kite), the camera footprint, the full map.

```

`docs/vp2_dof.md`:

```md
# VP2 depth of field (field post effect)

What the game computes for its depth of field, so the native renderer can draw it from the game's own data: the
packets the game builds for it (Z read as a texture, a T8 + CLUT channel shuffle, blur passes, a CT16 shuffle into
alpha, a final blend) are not drawn or read (no GS anywhere). Addresses are mod001 unless stated. [V] verified in the
MIPS, [I] inferred.

## The object

- A post-effect object of 0x13210 bytes, created at 0x222324 (ctor 0x2DAAA0), at `*(u32 *)0x0035DDF8 + 0x4448`. [V]
- Per-frame method 0x2D92A0 (vtable slot at main 0x159D90): allocates its work buffers, then calls the DOF manager
  0x2D21A0. [V]
- 0x2D21A0: auto-focus (byte `+0x130D0 == 1`) through 0x2D1820, else the parameter blocks `+0xF45C` (flag bit 1 of
  `+0xEF0C`) and `+0xF330 + i * 0x3C` (first word non-zero); each block goes to 0x2CCF60, which builds the packets. [V]

## Parameters (a 0x3C-byte block)

| Offset | Meaning |
|---|---|
| +0 | enable |
| +4..+0x14 | p0..p4: depth bytes (0..255) delimiting the blur ramps |
| +0x18 (s16) | blur level (0x80 / 0x5B / 0x40) |
| +0x1C (u8) | brightness factor c of the blurred copy (C = B x 2c / 128) |
| +0x1D..+0x23 | mode flags (+0x1F, +0x21: other table builders) |
| +0x24 / 28 / 2C / 30 | blur radii |
| +0x34 | strength 0..1 |

Depth scale `+0x131C4` by ZBUF PSM: Z32 16777216, Z24 65536, else 256: the table index is Z's top byte. [V]
Camera inputs (0x209A20, from the camera object `obj->0x94`): flags `+0xEF0C` from camera bytes +0x44B..+0x44F, mode
`+0x130D0` from +0x44D, focus `+0x130D4 / D8 / DC` from floats +0x450 / 454 / 458; projection constants `+0xF49C..F4BC`,
`+0x131C8` set by 0x2CB8D0 from camera +0x230 / 260 / 270 / 2A4 / 2B0. [V]

## The depth table (what to read)

0x2CC110 fills 256 entries at `this + 0xEF10`, one per depth byte: `a << 24 | v << 16 | v << 8 | v`, v the blurred
picture's weight (0..128, 128 = fully blurred), a an opacity ramp. [V arithmetic]

```
for i in 0..255:
  v = 0                                         if i > p0 or i < p4
    = 0.5 + 128 r (1 - (i - p1) / (p0 - p1))    if p1 < i <= p0
    = 0.5 + 128 table[i] / (|X(p2)| K)          if p2 < i <= p1     (table = *(float **)(this + 0x131B8))
    = 128                                       if p3 < i <= p2 (or p3 == p4)
    = 0.5 + 128 (i - p4) / (p3 - p4)            if p4 <= i <= p3
```

(X, K, r from the projection constants, see the research notes.) The native pass reads this finished table each frame:
it is the game's own data, built by its own code.

## The picture the game makes from it

Blur the frame at decreasing sizes (128x88 -> 64x44 -> 32x22, bilinear), brighten the blurred copy by 2c/128, then mix
it over the frame by the table's weight for each pixel's depth: `(blurred - frame) * w + frame`. Native: depth from the
native renderer's depth buffer, a blur of the native frame, one full-screen pass.

## What the title and the first field ask for (VP2S_POST_LOG and EE dumps, 2026-11-01)

Object at 0x5F18B0, flags `+0xEF0C` 0x1D, no auto-focus, the same from the title to the field. Corrections to the
table above: a block's words are +0x24 / +0x28 radii, **+0x2C / +0x30 blur gains (1.0), +0x34 s (0.0)**, +0x38 the
composite's Z; table entries are `a | v << 8 | v << 16 | a << 24` (R = A = a, G = B = v), a = 0x80 when s = 0. [V]
Before use c becomes `c + (128 - c) s` (for +0x21 != 0 only when flag bit 2 is set). [V]

| Block | +0x1C c | +0x18 / +0x1A | +0x1D..+0x23 | radii +0x24 / +0x28 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| +0xF45C | 0 | 0x80 | +0x1F 1: not drawn (flag bit 1 clear) | 0 / 0 |
| +0xF330 | 0x40 | 0x80 / 0x80 | +0x20 1, +0x21 0, +0x22 3 | 5.5 / 0 |
| +0xF36C | 0x40 | 0x80 / 0x80 | +0x20 1, +0x21 1, +0x22 3 | 5.5 / 0 |
| +0xF3A8 | 0xC8 | 0x80 / 0x40 | +0x20 1, +0x21 2 | 5.5 / 2.3 |
| +0xF3E4 | 0xB6 | 0x80 / 0x40 | +0x20 2, +0x21 3, +0x23 1 | 5.5 / 2.6 |
| +0xF420 | 0x80 | 0x80 / 0x40 | +0x20 2, +0x21 4, +0x23 1 | 5.5 / 2.6 |

## The picture of a block with +0x1F = 0 (0x2CCF60, every block above)

Buffers: the frame F (W x H = +0xA8 x +0xAC, 512 x 480); Z (PSMZ24); work maps of w x h = +0x131AC x +0x131B0
(128 x 89); C, the frame shrunk to w x h (made by another object, `*(0x35DDF8) + 0x444C`). Sizes: L18 / L1A the s16
+0x18 / +0x1A, h18 = L18 h / w. All sprites cover their rectangles exactly (pixel edges). [V]

1. Once a frame: Z point-sampled to w x 2h; index = min(255, Z24 >> (16 - n)), n = +0x131D0 (0 when +0xF4AC == 0). [V/I]
2. The block's table (2CC110 for +0x21 == 0, else 2CBBF0) as a CLUT; weight map S = table[index] (nearest, 2:1 in
   height), w x h. [V]
3. When L1A != w: S shrunk bilinear to L1A x h1A. When +0x28 > 0: blur 1 of that map, Gaussian sigma = 0.555 x
   (+0x28), gain +0x30. [V]
4. Colour: C stretched bilinear to L18 x h18 times g (RGBAQ from g = +0x131E0, 2.0 seen: min(255, C g)), then blur 2:
   Gaussian sigma = 0.555 x (+0x24) (5.5 -> 3.05 px at 128 wide), gain +0x2C. [V]
5. Alpha: the weight map's G scaled by 2c / 128 (c 0x40 = x1, 0xC8 = x3.1) to L18 x h18, clamped to 128. When c is
   0x40 and L1A == L18 that step is skipped and the alpha is taken from the blurred colour's green (a glow keyed by
   brightness; check at run time before relying on it). [V code, I meaning]
6. Composite over F (stretched bilinear to W x H): `out = F + (col - F) A / 128`, A times +0x1E / 128, only where the
   frame's Z <= +0x38 when (+0x20 & 3) == 1 (Z test GEQUAL with the sprite at Z +0x38 = 0: the cleared background,
   the sky). [V]

So on the title and the field the sky gets a blurred, ~2x brightened copy of the frame (the original's bright title
sky, drawn at vertex colour 64), and the blocks with +0x20 == 2 (no Z test) a brightness-keyed glow over everything.
Open: the VU1 blur microcode's handling of alpha, C's producer, the runtime values of +0x131C4..+0x131E0. [I]

## The frame's brightness (the other effect object, 2026-11-01)

The original's picture is twice as bright as the meshes draw it (vertex colour 64 = 1.0, the title sky): the object
at `*(0x35DDF8) + 0x4444` (vtable 0x159C90, per-frame method 0x2CB3E0, packets by 0x2C9040 / 0x2C8320) scales the
finished frame, before the interface: in mode 1 (+0xA8) `out = clamp(g x max(0, min(F, L0) - L1))`, L0 +0xAC, L1
+0xB0 (u32 0x00BBGGRR), g the RGB of +0xC0 = 2.0 (set by 0x2C8220 from the camera: 2 x 2^(cam+0x2D4), an exposure).
Modes 2 / 3 add tone bands (+0xB4 / +0xB8, gains +0xD0 / +0xE0) through work buffers. No blur, no glow. Found by
skipping each effect object's method on the GS comparison path (VP2S_POST_SKIP): only 0x2CB3E0 changes the title. [V]
The native port draws it as a levels pass (renderer PostEffect::levels).

## The field's orange haze: aerial perspective (the object at owner + 0x4450, 2026-11-01)

Per-frame method 0x2E0AE0 (vtable 0x159FA0), on when +0x18980 bit 0 (per area). A Rayleigh + Mie scattering pass: a
per-depth table (+0x134C4, built by 0x2DEFA0: R, G, B = 255 (1 - e^-tau), A = 128 e^-tau_avg) indexed by
min(255, Z24 >> (16 - n)) (n +0x18951), and an in-scatter colour V computed per 32-px grid vertex by the object's VU1
microcode (constants at *(+0x13AE4) + 0x70: q1 -sun, q2 camera, q3 Esun / betaTot, q4 betaR, q5 betaM, q7 / q8 the
phase constants, q9 k, q10 h). Per pixel `out = F x A x 127 / 128 / 128 + T.rgb x V / 128`. [V code / microcode
constants] Skipping it on the GS comparison path removes the field's orange; a fit of the two pictures gives
`out = 0.55..0.67 F + (86, 24, 23)` (keep factor = the table's A of near depths). The native pass (renderer
PostEffect::scatter) uses the game's table as it is and V from the formula with a gain of 3 calibrated on that fit;
the microcode itself is still to read (the formula alone gives a third of the in-scatter). [I]

```

`docs/vp2_models.md`:

```md
# VP2 models: what the native renderer draws from

VP2's draw code does not build vertices: for each visible mesh it puts a DMA REF to a prebuilt VIF packet inside the
model file (and one to a 16-qword material block) into the VIF1 chain. The geometry and the materials exist only as
those packets, so they are converted offline from the user's disc (rule decision 2026-10-31, as for the textures) and
loaded by the game's own pointer; everything else is read from the game's EE structures. Addresses are mod001. [V]
verified in the MIPS or the assets, [I] inferred.

## Objects

- Model objects: ctor 0x23F0A0 (base class vtable 0x154650, ~18 subclasses); vtable +0xFC draw 0x23A8D0, +0xF4
  0x23E820 (buffers). Per-frame update 0x23C640: 0x23E420 (flags), 0x23E050, 0x23CB20 (textures), 0x23A0F0 (cull and
  sort), then draw. [V]
- Fields: +0x90 world matrix (4 rows, translation in .w of rows 0..2); +0x220 the object's VU1 block (row r at
  +0x230 + 16 r: 9..12 the world matrix, 15.w fog, 16.z cull sign, 17 / 18 FBA / TEST); +0x644 resource (+0x8 data);
  +0x648 skeleton; +0x734 material palette (256 bytes an entry); +0x77C visible list; +0x7A4..7A8 group masks; +0x7BC
  model. [V]
- Model = the 'FPS\0' chunk + 0x10: +0x08 texture parts (FIS), +0x34 mesh count, +0x38 material table {count,
  qword offsets}, +0x57 lighting mode 0..3. First mesh at model + 0x60, next = cur + s16 at cur + 4. [V]
- Visible list (0x23A0F0, VU0 micro 0x1F0 / 0x188 culling): 12-byte entries {mesh, material block, depth}, opaque
  first, then blended meshes (strip tag ABE) by depth. Block = mesh + 0x40 when the mesh's flag bit 0 is set (or
  palette[idx - 1] when block + 0x5C is non-zero), else the previous mesh's. [V]
- Camera: camera + 0x650 / 0x690 / 0x6D0 = V / P / C (VU rows 48..59); 0x23A0F0 culls with camera + 0x2F0. [V packet,
  I roles]
- Skeleton S = obj[0x648]: bone count S[0x34][4], bone nodes S[0x3C][i] (world matrix at +0x90), per-bone matrices
  S[0x40] (bind inverse [I]), palette S[0x4C] + 0x40 + buf * n * 64 (double-buffered, buf = S[0x50]) filled by
  0x237020 (VU0 micro 0x898). [V layout]

## Meshes in the files (rmac, dnal, lfvw, lctp...: 24157 models, 335398 meshes)

One VIF packet a mesh, e.g.: UNPACK V4-16 x7 @0 (header), S-8 @0x16 (strip vertex counts), STCYCL 4/1, then per vertex
(4 qwords): +0 normal V3-32, +1 UV V2-16 (12-bit fixed, 4096 = 1), +2 colour V4-8 (128 = 1), +3 position V3-32.
Variants: V3-16 normal and position (set-up routine 8); constant UV / colour through STMASK / STROW; rigid skinning
with position V4-16, .w = VU bone address 0x290 + 4 * bone. Header: q0 (size, vertex base, strip count, strip list),
q1.xyz bounding centre, q2.x flags (bit 0 carries a material block, bit 3 forces the material path), q2.y vertex
count, q4 / q5 set-up routine data, q6.y group mask. [V]

Material block (16 qwords, VU rows 7..21): ALPHA_1, TEX0_1 templates, strip tags (PRIM, e.g. 0x5C tri-strip IIP TME
ABE), flags (.x cull off, .z texture index), untextured alternates. The texture is chosen by the table index at
block + 0x98 through 0x23CB20. [V]

## Native plan

- Offline (tools, output in build/, never committed): every mesh and material block of every model, keyed by where it
  is in the file (the asset part and offset).
- Runtime: the per-mesh loop of 0x23A8D0 (or the visible list of 0x23A0F0) gives the mesh and block pointers; where the
  game loaded that data gives the file and offset (its own disc reads and decompression, see port/vp2s_textures.cpp);
  the object's world matrix, the camera, the bones, the lights and the texture choice come from the structures above.
- Still to write natively from VU0 microcode: the culling (0x1F0 / 0x188) and the skinning product (0x898).

## Drawing from the game's data (the runtime spec)

[V] unless marked. The hook is the draw method 0x23A8D0: a0 the object, a2 the camera (the scene pass 0x2211E0 picks
`view = obj[0x80] ? obj[0x80] : scene[0x4080]`, camera = view[0x94]), a3 the render context. The object draws only when
`obj[0x7C3] & (1 << obj[0x7C5])`.

- Screen position: `s = M330 * V * O * (x, y, z, 1)` with O = obj+0x90 (4 rows, translation in .w of rows 0..2,
  applied M * p), V = cam+0x2F0, M330 = cam+0x330 (P transposed); X = s.x / s.w, Y = s.y / s.w (GS 12.4 space, 2048 the
  centre), Z = s.z / s.w (larger nearer). Clip: `c = M370 * V * O * p`, inside when |c.x|, |c.y|, |c.z| <= c.w. Fog
  (object row 15.w = obj+0x32C on): clamp(cam[0x798] + cam[0x79C] / s.w, 0, 255).
- Meshes: every mesh is in the visible list (nothing culled): with obj[0x7C2] != 0 and obj[0x77C] != 0, pointer i of
  obj[0x77C] + 12 n (n = model[0x34]) -> {mesh, block, depth}, opaque meshes first in file order, then blended ones
  far to near; else the meshes in file order. A mesh without its own block keeps the previous one's.
- Group masks (model[0x59] != 0), group g = u16 at mesh+0x36: g & obj[0x7A4] hides the mesh; g & obj[0x7A6] tints it
  by (obj[0x7E0], obj[0x7E4], obj[0x7E8], obj[0x7EC] * obj[0x7C4] / 128); g & obj[0x7A8] calls vtable +0xF8 for GS state.
- Material block rows (row r at blk + 0x10 + 16 (r - 7)): ALPHA blk+0x20, strip tag PRIM = (u32 blk+0x74 >> 15) &
  0x7FF (ABE bit 0x200000 of that word), cull flag blk+0x90, texture slot blk+0x98, CSA blk+0x9C; palette index
  blk+0x5C (1-based: the model's material table entry). Object fade (obj[0x210] != 0): ALPHA = *(0x314060 + 8 obj[0x210])
  with ABE set, back faces drawn.
- Texture by the game's key, not TEX0: G = *(u32 *)0x35DE38, e = *(s16 *)(G + 0x2670 + 2 slot), key = (tag *(u32 *)(G +
  0x1008 + 16 e), index *(u32 *)(G + 0x8 + 16 e)); written by 0x29D8F0 / 0x23DA80 during 0x23CB20, right before the draw.
- Object rows (row r at obj + 0x230 + 16 r): 1..4 light directions, 5..8 light colours, 13 object colour (= obj+0x7E0),
  14 lighting flags, 19 ambient; point and spot lights from the render context (ctx+0x210.., ctx+0x290..).
- Bones: n = *(u32 *)(S[0x34] + 4), matrix b at S[0x4C] + 0x40 + 64 (n * (u8)S[0x50] + b), row-vector form (p' = x R0 +
  y R1 + z R2 + R3; normals w = 0), giving object space. Routine 2: b = (position.w - 0x290) / 4; routine 4 entries the same.

```

`docs/vp2_vu1.md`:

```md
# VP2's VU1 microcode (native mesh path)

Notes for drawing VP2's 3D natively (TMB's mesh API: `setVif1Observer`, `setVu1Gate`, `Renderer`) instead of
running its VU1 microprograms and translating their GS packets. Read the code with `tools/vu_disasm.py` on images
dumped by `vp2s_vu1_replay <trace> dumpcode <dir>`.

## Which microprograms run (field, `build/vu1_traces/vp2_field_heavy.vu1rec`)

`vp2s_vu1_replay <trace> stats`: 18 (code image, start pc) pairs over 4000 runs.

| code image | pc | share of VU1 cycles |
|---|---|---|
| 02a71b3ea5e1c32f | 0x0880 | 70.4% |
| eff679a29e8d77d2 | 0x0880 | 14.7% |
| 02a71b3ea5e1c32f | 0x0770 | 4.2% |
| 5ee6d921ed2641b9 | 0x1690 | 2.8% (4 runs, 40k cycles each) |
| others | 0x06e0, 0x0238, 0x0678, 0x1410 | the rest |

The program at 0x0880 (two code images, likely two builds of the same library) is the mesh renderer.

## Program 0x0880 (code 02a71b3ea5e1c32f)

- `xtop vi14`: the object's block in the double buffer. Header words (qword index from vi14):
  - `0.y` vertex data offset (vi13), `0.w` strip-length list offset (vi12), `0.z` strip count (vi11)
  - `4.xyzw` / `5.xyzw`: up to four set-up routines (5.n = routine index, 4.n = its data offset), run by the
    subroutine at 0x2818 through `jalr` to instruction index 0x499 + n; then more at 0x54a + 2 * `8.y`
  - `13`: a qword copied into every strip's packet (vf30, written as `.yzw`: the GIF tag / register descriptor)
  - `15.x`: added to a counter (vi04), `mem[15].w` (global) selects the path at 0x0c38 (likely lit / different format)
- Globals (VU memory, absolute): `36.x/.y` the two output buffers, `41.z`, `68` (vf13), `81.w` the current output
  buffer, `85..88` a second matrix (vf09, vf27, vf29, vf12) used only for the clip test (`clipw`), `16.z` a sign used
  for back-face culling (cross product of successive edges, `opmula`/`opmsub`).
- Vertex: 4 qwords, stride 4 (`iaddiu vi13, vi13, 4`):
  - `+1` texture coordinates as 12-bit fixed point (`itof12`), multiplied by Q (1/w) → STQ
  - `+2` colour, stored as is (RGBAQ)
  - `+3` position, float xyz; transformed by vf01..vf04 (`mulax/madday/maddaz/maddw`), divided by w, `ftoi4` → XYZ
    12.4 (the matrix includes projection and viewport)
  - `+0` not read on this path
- Strips: the list at vi12 gives each strip's vertex count; a culled or clipped vertex gets the ADC bit (0x8000 in the
  XYZ word's w) so the GS skips its triangle.
- Output: per strip a GIF packet at vi10 (count, tag from header 13, then ST, RGBA, XYZ per vertex), sent with
  `xgkick`; the per-object state packet is kicked from vi07/vi08.

## Set-up routine 1 (0x3b58): rigid skinning

Each vertex's `+3.w` holds the VU address of a 4x4 bone matrix; the routine transforms the normal (`+0.xyz`, 3x3)
and the position (`+3.xyz`, 4x4) in place, for `2.y + 1` vertices. Routines 2-4 (0x3c78, 0x3d80, 0x3ec0) are
probably blends over more bones; the table at instruction 0x54a + 2n (0x2a50...) selects lighting modes, hence the
normal at `+0`.

## Which set-up routines objects use (field trace, 3000 runs)

Header `5.n` is an offset into the jump table at instruction 0x499 (two instructions an entry): 2 → 0x3b58 (rigid
skinning), 4 → 0x3c78, 6 → 0x3d80, 8 → 0x3ec0, 36, 46, 48 → further entries. Most common header 5 values:
(8, 4, 46, 0) 1180 + 149 runs, (8, 0, 0, 0) 1054, none 325, (8, 2, 0, 0) 99, (8, 4, 36, 46) 63, (6, 48, 0, 0) 54.
`8.y` (lighting table) is 0, 1 or 3; `mem[15].w` is 1 for almost every run.

The matrix vf01..vf04 is never changed by these runs: another microprogram loads it (likely the short ones at 0x0238
or 0x06e0 at the start of a frame), so the adapter reads it where that program does.

## Prototype check

`tools/vp2_mesh_proto.py <trace.vu1rec>` decodes every 0x0880 run from VU memory (positions after the set-up
routines, matrix vf01..vf04) and compares the screen positions with the XYZ the microprogram sent: field 2620 of
3000 runs match exactly (to the 1/16 pixel, z too), town 2982 of 3000. The rest is probably the 0x0c38 path.

## Output details (checked on all three traces)

- Set-up routines done natively: 8 (int normal 12-bit / position 12.4 to float), 6 (normals only), 2 (rigid
  skinning, count + 1 vertices), 4 (two-bone blend: per vertex x = matrix A, z = matrix B, y / w weights),
  48 (texture scroll: offset .zw at mem[38 + data.x], added to the 12-bit UV), 46 none. Not yet: 36, lighting mode 3,
  `mem[14].w != 0` paths.
- Lighting (table D, `mem[14].w == 0`): mode 0 colour times mem[13] (RGB to 0 without `mem[14].x`); mode 1 four
  directional lights: directions vf05..vf07 by component, colours mem[5..8], ambient mem[19], alpha mem[13].w,
  colour = clamp(vertex colour times light, 0, mem[0]), truncated. Some builds do not clamp the fourth light at 0
  (`maxx.xyz` + `move.w`): read from the code.
- Builds: the library exists at two layouts (set-up table 0x499 / 0x483, lighting table D 0x562 / 0x54c), found by
  following the BAL at 0x08a8 and its IADDIUs.
- XYZF2 w: ADC for a strip's first two vertices (don't care), any of the last three vertices outside the clip volume
  of mem[85..88], and back faces when the object culls (header `15.x + mem[41].z == 0`: cross product of the last
  three screen positions, sign alternating along the strip, positive = back); fog (`mem[15].w != 0`) =
  clamp(mem[68].z + mem[68].w * Q, 0, 255) in bits 4..11; an ADC vertex's w is the constant 0x8ff0.
- Packet: per strip a tag (x = vertex count, EOP on the last strip; y..w from header 13: PRIM 0x3C tri strip with
  IIP, TME, FGE, NREG 3, regs ST RGBAQ XYZF2), then ST (S, T, Q floats), RGBA (ints), XYZF2 per vertex.

`tools/vp2_mesh_proto.py` reproduces 180704 drawn vertices exactly (position to 1/16 pixel in 99.6% of the 32-bit
words, the rest 1 unit off; UV, colour, fog and ADC exact).

## Next

1. C++ high-level emulation of program 0x0880 in the port: at MSCAL, build the PATH1 packets natively and skip VU1
   (falling back to VU1 for what is not done yet), checked against the real VU1 run in a shadow mode.
2. Routine 36, lighting mode 3, the `mem[14].w != 0` paths.

```

`ghidra_scripts/AuditStats.java`:

```java
// Prints audit statistics for the loaded program: functions, instruction mix,
// VU0 macro-mode / MMI usage, and the biggest functions.
// @category VP2S

import java.util.*;
import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.*;

public class AuditStats extends GhidraScript {

    private static final Set<String> MMI_PREFIXES = Set.of(
        "padd", "psub", "pext", "ppac", "pcpy", "pmadd", "pmsub", "pmult", "pdiv",
        "pmfh", "pmth", "pmax", "pmin", "pceq", "pcgt", "pand", "por", "pxor", "pnor",
        "psll", "psrl", "psra", "pabs", "pinth", "pintel", "pexe", "prev", "prot", "plzcw",
        "qfsrv", "mfhi1", "mflo1", "mthi1", "mtlo1", "mult1", "multu1", "div1", "divu1", "madd1");

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        Listing listing = currentProgram.getListing();
        FunctionManager fm = currentProgram.getFunctionManager();

        println("Language: " + currentProgram.getLanguageID());
        println("Functions: " + fm.getFunctionCount());

        Map<String, Integer> mnem = new HashMap<>();
        long total = 0, vu0 = 0, mmi = 0, quad = 0, cop1 = 0;
        InstructionIterator it = listing.getInstructions(true);
        while (it.hasNext() && !monitor.isCancelled()) {
            String m = it.next().getMnemonicString().toLowerCase().replaceFirst("^_", "");
            mnem.merge(m, 1, Integer::sum);
            total++;
            if (m.startsWith("v") || m.equals("qmfc2") || m.equals("qmtc2") || m.equals("lqc2")
                    || m.equals("sqc2") || m.equals("cfc2") || m.equals("ctc2")) {
                vu0++;
            } else if (m.equals("lq") || m.equals("sq")) {
                quad++;
            } else if (m.endsWith(".s") || m.equals("lwc1") || m.equals("swc1")) {
                cop1++;
            } else {
                for (String p : MMI_PREFIXES) {
                    if (m.startsWith(p)) { mmi++; break; }
                }
            }
        }
        println(String.format("Instructions: %d | VU0 macro: %d | MMI: %d | lq/sq: %d | FPU: %d",
            total, vu0, mmi, quad, cop1));

        List<Map.Entry<String, Integer>> top = new ArrayList<>(mnem.entrySet());
        top.sort((a, b) -> b.getValue() - a.getValue());
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder("Top mnemonics:");
        for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(40, top.size()); i++) {
            sb.append(' ').append(top.get(i).getKey()).append('=').append(top.get(i).getValue());
        }
        println(sb.toString());

        List<Function> funcs = new ArrayList<>();
        fm.getFunctions(true).forEach(funcs::add);
        funcs.sort((a, b) -> Long.compare(b.getBody().getNumAddresses(), a.getBody().getNumAddresses()));
        println("Largest functions:");
        for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(15, funcs.size()); i++) {
            Function f = funcs.get(i);
            println(String.format("  %s %s %d bytes", f.getEntryPoint(), f.getName(), f.getBody().getNumAddresses()));
        }
    }
}

```

`ghidra_scripts/Callers.java`:

```java
// Decompiles the function containing an address, plus all of its direct callers.
// Args: <output file> <addr>
// @category VP2S

import java.io.PrintWriter;
import java.util.*;
import ghidra.app.decompiler.*;
import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Reference;

public class Callers extends GhidraScript {

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String[] args = getScriptArgs();
        Function target = getFunctionContaining(toAddr(args[1]));
        DecompInterface di = new DecompInterface();
        di.openProgram(currentProgram);
        Set<Function> funcs = new LinkedHashSet<>();
        funcs.add(target);
        for (Reference r : getReferencesTo(target.getEntryPoint())) {
            Function f = getFunctionContaining(r.getFromAddress());
            if (f != null) funcs.add(f);
        }
        try (PrintWriter out = new PrintWriter(args[0], "UTF-8")) {
            for (Function f : funcs) {
                DecompileResults res = di.decompileFunction(f, 120, monitor);
                out.println("// ===== " + f.getEntryPoint() + " " + f.getName()
                    + (f == target ? " (target)" : " (caller)"));
                out.println(res.decompileCompleted() ? res.getDecompiledFunction().getC() : "// failed");
            }
        }
        di.dispose();
        println("functions written: " + funcs.size());
    }
}

```

`ghidra_scripts/Decompile.java`:

```java
// Decompiles the functions at the given addresses into an output file.
// Args: <output file> <addr> [addr...]
// @category VP2S

import java.io.PrintWriter;
import ghidra.app.decompiler.*;
import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;

public class Decompile extends GhidraScript {

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String[] args = getScriptArgs();
        DecompInterface di = new DecompInterface();
        di.openProgram(currentProgram);
        try (PrintWriter out = new PrintWriter(args[0], "UTF-8")) {
            for (int i = 1; i < args.length; i++) {
                Function f = getFunctionAt(toAddr(args[i]));
                if (f == null) f = getFunctionContaining(toAddr(args[i]));
                if (f == null) {
                    // Routines only reached through pointers may be missing: create them (not saved with -readOnly)
                    disassemble(toAddr(args[i]));
                    f = createFunction(toAddr(args[i]), null);
                }
                if (f == null) { out.println("// no function at " + args[i]); continue; }
                DecompileResults r = di.decompileFunction(f, 120, monitor);
                out.println("// ===== " + f.getEntryPoint() + " " + f.getName());
                out.println(r.decompileCompleted() ? r.getDecompiledFunction().getC() : "// failed: " + r.getErrorMessage());
            }
        }
        di.dispose();
        println("wrote " + args[0]);
    }
}

```

`ghidra_scripts/FindKeyFunctions.java`:

```java
// Locates functions referencing key strings (file access) and the 'SLZ' magic constant.
// @category VP2S

import java.util.*;
import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.address.Address;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.*;
import ghidra.program.model.scalar.Scalar;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Reference;

public class FindKeyFunctions extends GhidraScript {

    private static final String[] STRINGS = {"pfs0:vp2.bin", "cdrom0:\\IOPRP300.IMG;1", "hdd0:PP.SLES-54645", "pfs0:/res"};
    // 'SLZ' as little-endian u32 prefix (0x005A4C53) and its upper half used by lui
    private static final long[] CONSTS = {0x5A4C53L, 0x5A4CL, 0x4C53L};

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        Listing listing = currentProgram.getListing();
        FunctionManager fm = currentProgram.getFunctionManager();

        for (String s : STRINGS) {
            Address a = find(null, s.getBytes("ASCII"));
            if (a == null) { println("string not found: " + s); continue; }
            Set<String> users = new TreeSet<>();
            for (Reference r : getReferencesTo(a)) {
                Function f = fm.getFunctionContaining(r.getFromAddress());
                users.add(f == null ? r.getFromAddress().toString() : f.getEntryPoint() + " " + f.getName());
            }
            println(String.format("'%s' @ %s used by %s", s, a, users));
        }

        Map<Long, Set<String>> hits = new HashMap<>();
        InstructionIterator it = listing.getInstructions(true);
        while (it.hasNext() && !monitor.isCancelled()) {
            Instruction ins = it.next();
            for (int op = 0; op < ins.getNumOperands(); op++) {
                Scalar sc = ins.getScalar(op);
                if (sc == null) continue;
                long v = sc.getUnsignedValue();
                for (long c : CONSTS) {
                    if (v == c) {
                        Function f = fm.getFunctionContaining(ins.getAddress());
                        hits.computeIfAbsent(c, k -> new TreeSet<>())
                            .add((f == null ? "?" : f.getEntryPoint().toString()) + ":" + ins.getAddress() + " " + ins);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        for (Map.Entry<Long, Set<String>> e : hits.entrySet()) {
            println(String.format("const 0x%X: %s", e.getKey(), e.getValue()));
        }
        if (hits.isEmpty()) println("no SLZ constant found in main ELF code");
    }
}

```

`ghidra_scripts/FindScalar.java`:

```java
// Lists every instruction using one of the given immediate values, grouped by function.
// Args: <hex value> [hex value...]
// @category VP2S

import java.util.*;
import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.*;
import ghidra.program.model.scalar.Scalar;

public class FindScalar extends GhidraScript {

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        Set<Long> wanted = new HashSet<>();
        for (String a : getScriptArgs()) wanted.add(Long.parseLong(a.replaceFirst("^0x", ""), 16));
        FunctionManager fm = currentProgram.getFunctionManager();
        InstructionIterator it = currentProgram.getListing().getInstructions(true);
        while (it.hasNext() && !monitor.isCancelled()) {
            Instruction ins = it.next();
            for (int op = 0; op < ins.getNumOperands(); op++) {
                Scalar s = ins.getScalar(op);
                // MIPS immediates are 16-bit and often sign-extended: also match on the low 16 bits
                if (s != null && (wanted.contains(s.getUnsignedValue()) || wanted.contains(s.getValue())
                        || wanted.contains(s.getValue() & 0xffffL))) {
                    Function f = fm.getFunctionContaining(ins.getAddress());
                    println(String.format("%s %s : %s", f == null ? "?" : f.getEntryPoint(), ins.getAddress(), ins));
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

```

`ghidra_scripts/FindSlzCandidates.java`:

```java
// Ranks functions likely to be the SLZ decompressor: immediate 'S','L','Z' compares,
// and LZ-loop shape (byte loads/stores, shifts, small masks).
// @category VP2S

import java.util.*;
import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.*;
import ghidra.program.model.scalar.Scalar;

public class FindSlzCandidates extends GhidraScript {

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        Listing listing = currentProgram.getListing();
        List<String[]> rows = new ArrayList<>();
        for (Function f : currentProgram.getFunctionManager().getFunctions(true)) {
            Set<Long> scalars = new HashSet<>();
            int lbu = 0, sb = 0, shifts = 0, masks = 0, total = 0, branchesBack = 0;
            for (Instruction ins : listing.getInstructions(f.getBody(), true)) {
                total++;
                String m = ins.getMnemonicString().toLowerCase().replaceFirst("^_", "");
                if (m.equals("lbu") || m.equals("lb")) lbu++;
                if (m.equals("sb")) sb++;
                if (m.startsWith("sll") || m.startsWith("srl") || m.startsWith("sra")) shifts++;
                for (int op = 0; op < ins.getNumOperands(); op++) {
                    Scalar s = ins.getScalar(op);
                    if (s == null) continue;
                    long v = s.getUnsignedValue();
                    scalars.add(v);
                    if (m.equals("andi") && (v == 0xf || v == 0xff || v == 0xfff || v == 0x3ff || v == 0x7ff || v == 0x1f)) masks++;
                }
                if (ins.getFlowType().isJump() && ins.getFlows().length > 0
                        && ins.getFlows()[0].compareTo(ins.getAddress()) < 0) branchesBack++;
            }
            boolean magic = scalars.contains(0x53L) && scalars.contains(0x4cL) && scalars.contains(0x5aL);
            int score = (magic ? 50 : 0) + Math.min(lbu, 20) + Math.min(sb, 20) + Math.min(shifts, 15)
                + masks * 3 + Math.min(branchesBack, 6) * 2;
            if (total < 20 || total > 2000) continue;
            rows.add(new String[]{String.format("%05d", score), f.getEntryPoint().toString(),
                String.format("score=%d magic=%s lbu=%d sb=%d shifts=%d masks=%d loops=%d size=%d",
                    score, magic, lbu, sb, shifts, masks, branchesBack, total)});
        }
        rows.sort((a, b) -> b[0].compareTo(a[0]));
        for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(15, rows.size()); i++) {
            println(rows.get(i)[1] + " " + rows.get(i)[2]);
        }
    }
}

```

`ghidra_scripts/PipelineExport.java`:

```java
// Runs PS2Recomp's ExportPS2Functions on the current program, deriving the output
// names from the program: <outdir>/<name>.toml and <outdir>/<name>_functions.csv
// (a trailing ".elf" is dropped). Lets one headless session export a whole batch.
// Args: <outdir>
// @category VP2S

import java.io.File;
import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;

public class PipelineExport extends GhidraScript {

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String outDir = getScriptArgs()[0];
        String base = currentProgram.getName().replaceFirst("\\.elf$", "");
        File toml = new File(outDir, base + ".toml");
        File csv = new File(outDir, base + "_functions.csv");
        runScript("ExportPS2Functions.java", new String[] { toml.getPath(), csv.getPath() });
        println("pipeline export: " + base + " -> " + toml.getPath());
    }
}
```
